4159               	;
4160               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4161               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4162               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4163               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4164               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4165               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4166               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4167               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4168               	; 	'
4169               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4170               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4171               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4172               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4173               	; 	'
4174               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4175               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4176               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4177               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4178               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4179               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4180               	; 	' anybody takes.
4181               	; 	'
4182               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4183               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4184               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4185               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.
4186               	; 	hsp4 = 7			' 1.75 px/frame, every Krook
4187 B89A 0200  20 	li r0,1792   ; 7*256

4211 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4212 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4213               	;
4214               	; 	' ======================================================================
4215               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4216               	; 	' ======================================================================
4217               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4218               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4219               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4220               	; 	' of running.
4221               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4222               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4223               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4224               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4225               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4226               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4227               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4228               	; 	'
4229               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4230               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4231               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4232               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4233               	; 	'
4234               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4235               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4236               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4237               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4238               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4239               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4240               	; 	'
4241               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4242               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4243               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4244               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4245               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4246               	; 	'
4247               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4248               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4249               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4250               	; draw_screen:
4251               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4252               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4253 B8D2 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8D4 A980     
4254 B8D6 E7C2     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4255               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4256 B8D8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8DA A980     
4257 B8DC BCC6     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4258               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4259 B8DE 04C0  14 	clr r0
4260 B8E0 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B8E2 2192     
4261               cv147
4262               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4263               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4264               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4265               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4266               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4267               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4268               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4269               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4270               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4271               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4272               	; 		WAIT
4273 B8E4 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8E6 A980     
4274 B8E8 A656     	data wait
4275               	; 		dlv = 3 - dq
4276 B8EA D020  34 	movb @cvb_DQ,r0
     B8EC 2192

4359 B9BE 0450  20 	b *r0
4360               	;
4361               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4362               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4363               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4364               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4365               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4366               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4367               	; 	'
4368               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4369               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4370               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4371               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4372               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4373               	; 	'
4374               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4375               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4376               	; 	' above it at all.
4377               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4378               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4379               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4380               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4381               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4382               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4383               	; 	'
4384               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4385               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4386               	; beam_one:
4387               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4388               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

4921               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
4922               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
4923               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
4924               	; 			'
4925               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4926               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4927               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4928               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4929               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4930               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4931               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4932               	; 			'
4933               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
4934               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
4935               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
4936               	; 			'
4937               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
4938               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5017               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5018               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5019               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5020               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5021               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5022               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5023               	; 			'
5024               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5025               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads the three
5026               	; 			' of them a screen-third apart so they arrive in sequence
5027               	; 			' instead of as one wall.
5028               	; 			stag = lx AND 63

     BEB4 21CA

     BEB8 3F00

     BF5E 225C     
5101 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5102               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5103               	; 			'
5104               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5105               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5106               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5107               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5108               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5109               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5110               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5111               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5112               	; 			'
5113               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5114               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5115               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5116               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5117               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5118               	; 			'
5119               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5120               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5121               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5122               	; 			'
5123               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5124               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5125               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5126               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5127               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5128               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5129               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5130 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21CA     
5131 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00     
5132 BF6A 0541  14 	inv r1
5133 BF6C 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
5134 BF6E C040  18 	mov r0,r1

     BF72 21BA

     C5F6 C694     
5906               cv292
5907               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5908               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5909               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5910               	; 		'
5911               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5912               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5913               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5914               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5915               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5916               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5917               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5918               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5919               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5920               	; 		'
5921               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5922               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5923               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5924               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5925               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5926               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
5927 C5F8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6556               	;
6557               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6558               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6559               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6560               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6561               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6562               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6563               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6564               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6565               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6566               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6567               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6568               	; 	'
6569               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6570               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6571               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6572               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6573               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7132               	;
7133               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7134               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7135               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7136               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7137               	; 	'
7138               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7139               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7140               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7141               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7142               	; 	'
7143               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7144               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7145               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7146               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7147               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7148               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7149               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7150               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7151               	; 	'
7152               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7153               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7154               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7155               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7156               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7157               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7158               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7159               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7160               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7161               	; 	'
7162               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7163               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7164               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7165               	; wipe_shelf:
7166               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7167               	; 	wsn = 8

7238               	;
7239               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7240               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7241               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7242               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7243               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7244               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7245               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7246               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7247               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7248               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7249               	; 	' nothing.
7250               	; 	'
7251               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7252               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7253               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7254               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
7255               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to

7311               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
7312               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
7313               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
7314               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
7315               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
7316               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7317               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7318               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
7319               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
7320               	; 	'
7321               	; 	' So it tests for the thing it is allowed to remove rather than trusting
7322               	; 	' the position. COUNTR is a pillar or a counter; anything else stays.

8238               	;
8239               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8240               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8241               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8242               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8243               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8244               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8245               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8246               	; 	'
8247               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8248               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8249               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8250               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8251               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8252               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8253               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8254               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8255               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8256               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8257               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8258               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8259               	; 	'
8260               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8261               	; 	'
8262               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8263               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8264               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8265               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8266               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8267               	; 	'
8268               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
8269               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
8270               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every

8802 DB6C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8803 DB6E 0450  20 	b *r0
8804               	;
8805               	; 	' ======================================================================
8806               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8807               	; 	' ======================================================================
8808               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8809               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8810               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8811               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8812               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8813               	; 	'
8814               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8815               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8816               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8817               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8818               	; 	' broken.
8819               	; 	'
8820               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8821               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8822               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8823               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8824               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8825               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8826               	; 	'
8827               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8828               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8829               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8830               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8831               	; coll_obst:
8832               cvb_COLL_OBST
8833               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8834 DB70 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DB72 218E

     DB76 0300

     DB7E DB84

9130               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,

9132               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
9133               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
9134               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
9135               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
9136               	; 	'
9137               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
9138               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.

9368               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
9369               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
9370               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9371               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9372               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9373               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9374               	; 	'
9375               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9376               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9377               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9378               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.
9379               	; 	IF klx > hx THEN hdd = klx - hx ELSE hdd = hx - klx

9413               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
9414               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
9415               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
9416               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
9417               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.

9441               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9442               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9443               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9444               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9445               	; 	'
9446               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9447               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9448               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9449               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9450               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9451               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9452               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
9453               	; 	ky = flry(klv)
9454 E07E D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLV,r0

10355 E832 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10356 E834 0450  20 	b *r0
10357               	;
10358               	; 	' ======================================================================
10359               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10360               	; 	' ======================================================================
10361               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10362               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10363               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10364               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10365               	; 	'
10366               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10367               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10368               	; 	'
10369               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10370               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10371               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10372               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10373               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10374               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10375               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10376               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10377               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10378               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10379               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10380               	; esc_tick:
10381               cvb_ESC_TICK
10382               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10383               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10384               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10385               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10386               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10387 E836 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E838 217C     
10388 E83A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E83C 0100     
10389 E83E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10390 E840 1402  14 	jhe cv782
10391 E842 0460  28 	b @cv781
     E844 E85A     
10392               cv782
10393               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10394 E846 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E848 217C     
10395 E84A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E84C 0700     
10396 E84E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10397 E850 1A02  14 	jl cv784
10398 E852 0460  28 	b @cv783
     E854 E85A     
10399               cv784
10400 E856 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10401 E858 0450  20 	b *r0
10402               cv783
10403               	; 	END IF
10404               cv781
10405               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10406               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10407               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10408               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10409               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10410               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10411               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10412               	; 	'
10413               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10414               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10415               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10416               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10417               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10418               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10419               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10420               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10421 E85A D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E85C 20B0     
10422 E85E 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E860 0100

     E864 20B0

10433               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10434               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10435               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10436               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10437               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10438               	; 	'
10439               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10440               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10441               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10442               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10443               	; 	'
10444               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10445               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10446               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10447               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10448               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10449               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10450               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10451               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.

10453               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never

10714 EAB4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10715 EAB6 0450  20 	b *r0
10716               	;
10717               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
10718               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
10719               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
10720               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
10721               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
10722               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
10723               	; 	'
10724               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
10725               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
10726               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
10727               	; 	'
10728               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
10729               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
10730               	; 	'   +2  GREY
10731               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
10732               	; 	'
10733               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
10734               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
10735               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
10736               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
10737               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
10738               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
10739               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
10740               	; 	'
10741               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
10742               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
10743               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
10744               	; 	'
10745               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
10746               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
10747               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10748               	; scan_furn:
10749               cvb_SCAN_FURN
10750               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3

11165 EE0A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11166 EE0C 0450  20 	b *r0
11167               	;
11168               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11169               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11170               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11171               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11172               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11173               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11174               	; scan_wipe:
11175               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11176               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11177               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11178               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11179               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11180               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11181               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11182               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11183               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11184               	; 	'
11185               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11186               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11187               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11188               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11189               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11190               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11191               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11192               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11193               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11194               	; 	'
11195               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11196               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11197               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11198               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11199               	; 	'
11200               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11201               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11202               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11203 EE0E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EE10 1680     
11204 EE12 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     EE14 20D6     
11205               	; 	FOR swj = 0 TO 5
11206 EE16 04C0  14 	clr r0

11273               	;
11274               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11275               	; 	'
11276               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11277               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11278               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11279               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11280               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11281               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11282               	; 	'
11283               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11284               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11285               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11286               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11287               	; 	'
11288               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11289               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11290               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11291               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11292               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11293               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11294               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11295               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11296               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11297               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11298               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11299               	; 	say = 3 - elvl

11964               	; 	' THE CAST STAYS ON SCREEN FOR THE COUNT. Hiding everything first threw

12004               	;
12005               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12006               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12007               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12008               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12009               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12010               	; 	'
12011               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12012               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12013               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12014               	; 	'
12015               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12016               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12017               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12018               	; 	'
12019               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12020               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12021               	; bonus_count:
12022               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
12023               	; 	#bval = 100

     F55A F4AC     
12124               	;
12125               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12126               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12127               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12128               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12129               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12130               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12131               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12132               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12133               	; 	'
12134               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12135               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12136               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12137               	;
12138               	; do_escape:

12140               	; 	escapd = 0

     F560 20FC

     F68A AA16     
12272               	;
12273               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12274               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12275               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12276               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12277               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12278               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12279               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12280               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12281               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12282               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12283               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12284               	; snd_off:
12285               cvb_SND_OFF

12325               	; 	' AND THE EFFECTS THAT HAVE NOT HAPPENED YET. A set sf* flag is a sound
12326               	; 	' waiting for the next pass of the main loop -- and between a capture and
12327               	; 	' the next Krook the main loop does not run, so anything latched during
12328               	; 	' the round (a hit as Harry was caught, the bonus Kop the tally just
12329               	; 	' awarded) survived the silence and fired on the first pass of the NEW
12330               	; 	' round. Silencing the channels does not help: the flag plays a fresh
12331               	; 	' note afterwards, with a fresh decay counter, which is why it came out
12332               	; 	' as one long tone over the start of a level that had earned nothing.

12382               	;
12383               	; 	' ======================================================================
12384               	; 	' SOUND
12385               	; 	' ======================================================================
12386               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12387               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12388               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12389               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12390               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
12391               	; sfx_tick:
12392               cvb_SFX_TICK

     FA2E FA5E     
12714               cv960
12715               	; 		spt = spt - 1
12716 FA30 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SPT,r0
     FA32 2204     
12717 FA34 0220  22 	ai r0,65280	; -1
     FA36 FF00

     FA3A 2204

12720 FA3C 04C1  14 	clr r1
12721 FA3E 9001  18 	cb r1,r0

12724               cv962

     FA4E A980

     FA56 D000

12737 FA5E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12738 FA60 0450  20 	b *r0
12739               	;
12740               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12741               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12742               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12743               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12744               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12745               	; 	'
12746               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12747               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12748               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12749               	; 	'
12750               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12751               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12752               	; 	' letter after it.
12753               	;
12754               	; font_bits:
12755               cvb_FONT_BITS
12756               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
12757 FA62 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     FA64 0000

12885               	bank 3
12886               	; 	#endif
12887               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12888               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12889               	; 	'
12890               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12891               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12892               	; 	' being small sprites.
12893               	; 	'
12894               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12895               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12896               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12897               	; 	'
12898               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12899               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12900               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12901               	;
12902               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12903               cvb_SPR_KELLY

13735               	;
13736               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
13737               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
13738               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
13739               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
13740               	;
13741               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
13742               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
13743               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
13744               	; 	' unreadable.
13745               	;
13746               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
13747               cvb_SCAN_COL3
13748               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
13749 6C4A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     6C4C EEEE     
     6C4E ECEC     
     6C50 ECBC     
13750               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$EC,$EC,$BC,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
13751 6C52 ECEC     	byte >ec,>ec,>ec,>bc,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     6C54 ECBC     
     6C56 ECEC     
     6C58 ECBC     
13752               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$EC,$EC,$BC,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EE
13753 6C5A ECEC     	byte >ec,>ec,>ec,>bc,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ee
     6C5C ECBC     
     6C5E EEEE     
     6C60 EEEE

13849               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13850               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13851               	; 	'
13852               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13853               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13854               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13855               	; 	'
13856               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13857               	; 	' blit is
13858               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13859               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13860               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13861               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13862               	; 	'
13863               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13864               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13865               	; 	'
13866               	; 	' template ids:
13867               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13868               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13869               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13870               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13871               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13872               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13873               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13874               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13875               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13876               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13877               	;
13878               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13879               cvb_STOR_TPL
13880               	; 	DATA BYTE 151,151,151,151,107,151,151,151,151,151,151,107,151,151,151,151
